I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't think I'm depressed or... anxious. Just... frustrated with myself, I guess. But no.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty completing tasks at home due to these memory lapses?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose. I'm not really sure. I guess it just depends on what it is. I haven't really kept track of how long I focus on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... that's kind of the main reason I came in. At work—on the floor—I just... I lose my train of thought constantly. It's just... it's been a problem. \n\nAt work, the concerns have gotten serious enough that... well, they actually put me on formal supervision. The other nurses are having to double-check parts of my follow-through when I'm charge nurse. It's... yeah. It's not just at the hospital. \n\nI guess I just forget things that should be... routine. Like paying a bill at home, or... calling a family member back. I'll mean to do it, and I just don't. Sometimes I even miss appointments after I've set a reminder on my phone. Or, you know, forgetting to follow up on a missing order... it just slips my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't think so. I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't think I'm depressed or... anxious. Just... frustrated with myself, I guess. But no.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty completing tasks at home due to these memory lapses?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose. Sometimes things just don't get finished. But... I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on a single task for an extended period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I guess it just depends on what it is. I haven't really kept track of how long I focus on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... that's kind of the main reason I came in. At work—on the floor—I just... I lose my train of thought constantly. Like, if an IV pump beeps, or... a call light goes off, or even if someone just asks another nurse a question... suddenly I've just completely forgotten what patient flow issue I was working on. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of sadness or irritability?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't... I haven't really noticed feeling sad or anything like that. Just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed by your forgetfulness and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess so. It's just... it's been a problem. \n\nAt work, the concerns have gotten serious enough that... well, they actually put me on formal supervision. The other nurses are having to double-check parts of my follow-through when I'm charge nurse. It's... yeah. I just feel like I'm becoming a liability instead of a leader.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess I haven't really thought about it like that. I'm just... trying to get through my shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is exhausted after a long day?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I mean, I'm just tired in general. Working a full shift on the floor is... it's just tiring. I haven't really separated it out like that. I just go home and try to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to think clearly after a long day at work?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. Like I said, I'm just worn out by the time my shift is over. I haven't really noticed if my thinking is worse right then... I just try to get home and rest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I interact with my patients and the other nurses all day. I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No... I've never experienced anything like that. I just feel like myself, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'm not really sure. I guess it just depends on what it is. I haven't really kept track of how long I focus on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... that's kind of the main reason I came in. At work—on the floor—I just... I lose my train of thought constantly. Like, if an IV pump beeps, or... a call light goes off, or even if someone just asks another nurse a question... suddenly I've just completely forgotten what patient flow issue I was working on. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of sadness or irritability?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't... I haven't really noticed feeling sad or anything like that. Just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed by your forgetfulness and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess so. It's just... it's been a problem. \n\nAt work, the concerns have gotten serious enough that... well, they actually put me on formal supervision. The other nurses are having to double-check parts of my follow-through when I'm charge nurse. It's... yeah. I just feel like I'm becoming a liability instead of a leader.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess I haven't really thought about it like that. I'm just... trying to get through my shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is exhausted after a long day?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I mean, I'm just tired in general. Working a full shift on the floor is... it's just tiring. I haven't really separated it out like that. I just go home and try to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to think clearly after a long day at work?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. Like I said, I'm just worn out by the time my shift is over. I haven't really noticed if my thinking is worse right then... I just try to get home and rest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I interact with my patients and the other nurses all day. I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No... I've never experienced anything like that. I just feel like myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I get tired, but... I don't think I'm ever in a daze. I just... keep doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble meeting your responsibilities at home because of your memory lapses and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. Sometimes things just don't get finished. But... I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on a single task for an extended period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I guess it just depends on what it is. I haven't really kept track of how long I focus on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... that's kind of the main reason I came in. At work—on the floor—I just... I lose my train of thought constantly. Like, if an IV pump beeps, or... a call light goes off, or even if someone just asks another nurse a question... suddenly I've just completely forgotten what patient flow issue I was working on. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of sadness or irritability?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't... I haven't really noticed feeling sad or anything like that. Just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed by your forgetfulness and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess so. It's just... it's been a problem. \n\nAt work, the concerns have gotten serious enough that... well, they actually put me on formal supervision. I just feel like I'm becoming a liability instead of a leader.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess I haven't really thought about it like that. I'm just... trying to get through my shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is exhausted after a long day?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I mean, I'm just tired in general. Working a full shift on the floor is... it's just tiring. I haven't really separated it out like that. I just go home and try to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to think clearly after a long day at work?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. Like I said, I'm just worn out by the time my shift is over. I haven't really noticed if my thinking is worse right then... I just try to get home and rest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I interact with my patients and the other nurses all day. I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No... I've never experienced anything like that. I just feel like myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I get tired, but... I don't think I'm ever in a daze. I just... keep doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble meeting your responsibilities at home because of your memory lapses and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. I just... I forget things that should be routine. Like paying a bill at home, or... calling a family member back. Missing an appointment. I just forget.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact on your personal relationships due to your memory lapses and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so.
